# FINAL DRAFT REV 4 — Joint ACMS + Server Manager + PDU Manager Large Autonomous Sprint Plan
## LLM Manager SQLAlchemy Migration, Agent Runtime Manager Split, Hermes Durable State Backup, PDU API Integration, and Cross-Service Validation

**Date:** 2026-09-27  
**Primary execution agent:** STEA-004 Entrepreneur  
**Repositories:**
- `startupteams/llm-manager-project-framework`
- `startupteams/acms-project-framework`
- `startupteams/pdu-marion-ia-usa-project-framework`
- `startupteams/hermes-base-setup`

## 1. Human-approved architecture decisions

The following are approved and should be treated as current direction.

### Server Manager structure

```text
Server Manager
├── LLM Manager
│   ├── models
│   ├── inference/routing
│   ├── deployment revisions
│   ├── benchmarks/qualification
│   └── raw inference/cost
│
└── Agent Runtime Manager
    ├── VM/container runtime lifecycle
    ├── provisioning
    ├── runtime desired/actual state
    ├── runtime health/recovery
    ├── harness bootstrap
    ├── Agent Bridge bootstrap
    └── keepalive/restart
```

Both remain in the same repository initially. Separate repository/hosting is future technical debt.

### Language/framework

Use:

```text
Python 3.12+
FastAPI
Pydantic
PostgreSQL
SQLAlchemy 2.x
Alembic
pytest
```

Python/FastAPI is the approved default now. Re-evaluate Go/Rust only if later evidence justifies it.

### Databases

Use separate logical databases/service ownership:

```text
ACMS database
  ACMS only

llmmanager database
  LLM Manager only

agent_runtime_manager database
  Agent Runtime Manager only
```

They may share the same PostgreSQL host/cluster initially.

Use separate credentials and no cross-database foreign keys.

Cross-service correlation uses UUIDs/APIs.

### Persistence migration

Perform a **full migration to SQLAlchemy 2.x + Alembic** for the authoritative LLM Manager and Agent Runtime Manager data layers.

The migration must be staged and behavior-preserving.

### UI

Keep the existing server-rendered UI functional.

Do not spend this sprint rewriting the frontend.

Machine API boundaries must be separated from human UI routes.

### Kubernetes

Deferred.

The first ACMS/Server Manager proof uses the existing Ubuntu 24.04 VM + Hermes approach.

### ACMS persistent identity

ACMS creates/reserves the durable agent identity.

Agent Runtime Manager creates a replaceable runtime identity underneath it.

### First ACMS-managed worker

Do **not** destroy, repurpose, or use the existing STEA-004 Entrepreneur VM as the test worker.

Create a new VM that mimics the proven STEA-004/Hermes runtime pattern.

STEA-004 Entrepreneur remains the development/executive agent driving this sprint.

### Production/autonomy

The implementation agent is authorized to work autonomously across the approved scope.

Default repository/release documentation may continue to say production requires human approval, but this sprint's execution context may explicitly grant that approval so STEA-004 can proceed autonomously through deployment gates when all required checks pass.

---

## 2. Mandatory pre-change protection

Before substantial LLM Manager refactoring or database migration:

1. identify the Proxmox/ZFS storage backing the current Server Manager/LLM Manager deployment;
2. create a **ZFS snapshot of the current known-good state**;
3. record:
   - snapshot name;
   - timestamp;
   - VM/volume covered;
   - current Git SHA;
   - DB schema/migration version;
   - current service health;
4. verify the snapshot exists;
5. preserve existing DB backup/release rollback procedures as an additional recovery layer.

Suggested snapshot naming:

```text
pre-server-manager-refactor-20260927-<timestamp>
```

Do not proceed with destructive refactor/migration until the snapshot and normal DB/application rollback paths are verified.

---

## 3. Sprint objective

Execute as much of the following as safely possible without waiting for additional human direction unless a stop condition is reached:

1. finish remaining LLM Manager CI/CD/release work;
2. snapshot and preserve current production state;
3. refactor LLM Manager into the approved modular stack;
4. fully migrate authoritative persistence to SQLAlchemy/Alembic;
5. create Agent Runtime Manager as a peer service in the same repository;
6. move runtime/provisioning state into its own database;
7. isolate Proxmox VM operations behind a runtime-provider abstraction;
8. create a versioned Server Manager machine API;
9. implement scoped ACMS service authentication;
10. develop paired Server Manager and ACMS PRs;
11. create a new `acms-worker-001` VM using the proven Ubuntu 24.04/Hermes approach;
12. bootstrap Agent Bridge;
13. register using the ACMS-created persistent identity;
14. perform real work/steering/handoff tests;
15. test runtime restart/reconnect;
16. enforce per-agent Hermes Git branch/state backup in `startupteams/hermes-base-setup`;
17. integrate the existing PDU Manager `/api/v1` safely with ACMS and Server Manager;
18. create reusable PDU client integration and service identities;
19. verify cross-service correlation from ACMS/Server Manager operations into PDU audit;
20. document architecture debt, test results, production state, and remaining questions.

Kubernetes is explicitly deferred.

---

## 4. Phase 0 — complete release foundation

Before refactoring:

- rotate already-identified credentials;
- finish self-hosted deploy runner;
- prove automatic staging deployment;
- perform/prove protected production promotion;
- convert/verify VM114 `releases/<sha>` + `current`;
- prove rollback;
- run staging/production parity;
- update operations docs.

Gate:

```text
known production SHA
known DB state
known ZFS snapshot
staging reproducible
rollback verified
health checks reproducible
```

## 5. Phase 1 — refactor LLM Manager

Use a staged/strangler approach.

Target organization:

```text
server_manager/
├── common/
│   ├── config/
│   ├── auth/
│   ├── db/
│   ├── audit/
│   └── contracts/
│
├── llm_manager/
│   ├── api/
│   ├── models/
│   ├── schemas/
│   ├── services/
│   ├── repositories/
│   ├── collectors/
│   ├── routing/
│   └── recovery/
│
└── agent_runtime_manager/
    ├── api/
    ├── models/
    ├── schemas/
    ├── services/
    ├── repositories/
    ├── providers/
    │   └── proxmox_vm.py
    └── bootstrap/
```

For each extraction:

```text
existing behavior
→ new module/service
→ tests
→ staging parity
→ route existing behavior through new module
→ remove old path only after parity
```

Avoid a single big-bang cutover.

## 6. Phase 2 — full SQLAlchemy/Alembic migration

### LLM Manager

Migrate authoritative current model/inference/deployment/benchmark/usage/cost data to SQLAlchemy-backed persistence.

Use:

```text
SQLAlchemy ORM
SQLAlchemy Core / explicit SQL when clearer
Alembic as schema migration authority
```

### Agent Runtime Manager

Create the new `agent_runtime_manager` database and migrate only authoritative runtime-management data.

Every current AgentManager table must be classified:

```text
KEEP
MIGRATE
DEPRECATE
REFERENCE-ONLY
DELETE-LATER
```

Expected direction:

**Likely runtime-owned**
- agents/runtime identity mapping
- runtime-administration access
- provisioning_jobs
- provisioning_steps
- runtime audit

**Likely ACMS semantic overlap / not canonical in Runtime Manager**
- projects
- work_items
- runs
- plans
- sessions
- messages

Do not silently preserve semantic duplication merely because the old schema contains it.

For each authoritative table verify:

- row counts;
- key preservation;
- selected checksums/content;
- staging restore;
- API smoke;
- rollback notes.

---

## 7. Phase 3 — establish Agent Runtime Manager

Agent Runtime Manager becomes a peer deployable FastAPI service.

Own:

```text
runtime ID
acms_agent_id correlation
provider
VMID/workload identity
desired runtime state
actual runtime state
health
provisioning job
restart/recreate history
harness/runtime version
Agent Bridge bootstrap metadata
model-route reference
runtime audit
```

Do not own semantic work/project/context history.

---

## 8. Runtime provider and destructive permissions

Initial provider:

```text
ProxmoxVMProvider
```

Agent Runtime Manager may create and destroy VMs **only when those VMs were created/claimed by Agent Runtime Manager and carry durable ownership metadata proving that fact**.

Never destroy:

- STEA-004 Entrepreneur / VM906;
- arbitrary pre-existing Proxmox VMs;
- model-serving VMs;
- infrastructure VMs;
- a VM whose ownership marker cannot be verified.

Persist at minimum:

```text
server_runtime_id
acms_agent_id
provisioning_request_id
provider
node
vmid
created_by_agent_runtime_manager = true
created_at
template/source
```

Add a recognizable Proxmox description/tag if practical.

Before destructive deletion of a Runtime-Manager-owned VM:

1. verify ownership;
2. verify it is not protected/pinned;
3. preserve required work/state according to the runtime persistence policy;
4. create/verify a ZFS snapshot when policy requires preservation;
5. record an audit event;
6. stop cleanly where possible;
7. destroy the VM and its disposable storage rather than accumulating unlimited stopped trial VMs;
8. keep the ACMS persistent identity/history unless ACMS separately retires it.

`Destroy runtime` is not the same operation as `Delete persistent agent`.

## 10. Phase 4 — Server Manager API v1

Infrastructure API is distinct from A2A.

Recommended initial contract:

```text
POST   /api/v1/agent-runtimes
GET    /api/v1/provisioning-jobs/{job_id}
GET    /api/v1/agent-runtimes/{runtime_id}
POST   /api/v1/agent-runtimes/{runtime_id}/desired-state
DELETE /api/v1/agent-runtimes/{runtime_id}
GET    /api/v1/model-routes/{route}
GET    /api/v1/usage
```

`DELETE` applies only to Runtime Manager-owned runtimes and must not mean "delete ACMS persistent agent."

Creation requires:

```text
acms_agent_id
request_id/idempotency key
name
harness
runtime_class
site
model_profile/route
desired_state
bridge_profile
```

## 10A. Hermes durable state / Git backup requirement

Every newly created Hermes worker must receive a dedicated branch in:

```text
startupteams/hermes-base-setup
```

The current repository already supports the basic pattern:

```text
init_employee.py
  -> creates a dedicated profile
  -> creates a dedicated branch
  -> commits profile files
  -> pushes the branch

scripts/sync_memory.sh
  -> refuses to auto-sync main/master
  -> syncs tracked profile/skills content
  -> creates periodic commits
  -> pushes the current worker branch
```

The repository's current `.gitignore` is fail-closed and already whitelists core Markdown/skills/memories paths while blocking obvious secret-bearing artifacts. The sprint must preserve and strengthen that model.

### Required runtime provisioning behavior

When Agent Runtime Manager creates a Hermes worker:

1. allocate/record the ACMS persistent agent ID;
2. derive a stable Hermes profile/branch name;
3. create or adopt a dedicated Git branch;
4. initialize the Hermes profile from an approved template;
5. ensure the worker checks out only its assigned branch;
6. install automated Git synchronization;
7. verify a non-secret state change can be committed and pushed;
8. record the branch and repository in Agent Runtime Manager runtime metadata;
9. report the branch/state-backup status to ACMS.

Suggested metadata:

```text
hermes_state_repo = startupteams/hermes-base-setup
hermes_state_branch
hermes_profile_name
last_state_commit_sha
last_state_push_at
state_sync_health
```

### Content intended for cloud Git backup

Persist versionable, non-secret Hermes state such as:

```text
SOUL.md
USER.md
MEMORY.md
other approved Markdown context/handoff files
skills/
memories/
approved profile configuration that contains no secret values
agent bootstrap/version metadata
```

The implementation must inspect actual Hermes paths and repository ignore behavior before assuming a file is tracked.

### Never commit

```text
passwords
PATs
GitHub recovery codes
API keys
Telegram bot tokens
OpenRouter/LLM keys
private SSH keys
session cookies
runtime databases containing secrets
logs containing credentials
provider secrets
```

A pre-commit/state-sync secret scan should run before push.

### Sync behavior

The current 15-minute cron model is acceptable as the initial implementation and should be treated as technical debt rather than the final cloud-state architecture.

Required properties:

- lock to prevent overlapping sync runs;
- only operate on the worker's dedicated branch;
- pull/rebase safely before push;
- no-change runs exit successfully;
- commit message includes agent/branch and timestamp;
- push failure is visible in runtime health/telemetry;
- never switch to or commit directly onto `main`;
- never push secret material merely because it is not currently ignored.

### State backup is not the entire ACMS context model

Git backup protects Hermes durable persona/skills/Markdown state.

ACMS separately remains authoritative for:

```text
Work Items
assignment
approved context
task/session correlation
handoffs
artifacts
audit
human direction
```

At task completion, ACMS should ensure the latest handoff/artifact references are captured even if the periodic Git sync has not run yet.

### Technical debt record — Hermes Git state sync

Create a TDR documenting:

> Per-agent Git branches and periodic commits to `hermes-base-setup` are the current cloud backup mechanism for Hermes agent state.

Future replacement candidates may include:

```text
dedicated Agent State service
object storage
versioned workspace snapshots
Git/Bitbucket abstraction
managed artifact/context storage
hybrid Git + object storage
```

Any replacement must preserve inspectability, version history, rollback, and per-agent isolation.

### GitHub machine identity

Do not encode interactive GitHub account passwords or 2FA recovery codes in any repository, VM image, provisioning record, script, or handoff.

For the initial worker, Agent Runtime Manager should inject a repository-scoped non-interactive credential from external secret storage. Prefer, in order:

```text
GitHub App installation token
or
human-created machine user + fine-grained PAT / SSH key
or
repo-scoped deploy credential where write semantics fit
```

The credential must be rotatable independently of the VM.

Per-agent attribution should primarily use:

```text
dedicated branch
agent-specific git author name/email
ACMS agent ID in commit metadata where useful
```

rather than requiring a distinct GitHub login account for every agent.

Create a TDR for the initial shared/machine automation identity if used. Future work should evaluate a GitHub App or internal credential broker for per-agent scoped tokens.

## 10B. PDU Manager becomes a first-class cross-service dependency

The PDU Manager is **not** a greenfield API project.

Production is:

```text
VM156 on MIAM-00133
https://10.0.20.156/
API root: /api/v1
```

VM154 is powered-off fallback.

The existing API already has important safety primitives:

```text
LLDAP authentication
authorization
protected-outlet policy
idempotency
asynchronous jobs
per-PDU serialization
rate limiting
batch validation
audit
state verification
```

Do not create another service that talks directly to the PowerAlert PDUs.

The PDU Manager remains the **sole physical-power authority**.

### Revised integration architecture

```text
Independent authorized agent ───────────────┐
                                            │ scoped PDU API
ACMS ───────────────────────────────────────┼────> PDU Manager API
  │                                         │
  └──> Agent Runtime Manager ───────────────┤
                                            │
LLM Manager ──> Server Manager/ARM ─────────┘
                         |
                         v
                    PDU Manager
                         |
                         v
                 physical PDU outlets
```

The latest human direction permits ACMS to interface with PDU Manager directly for ACMS-controlled agents.

Therefore:

### ACMS

ACMS may directly:

```text
read PDU/service health
read asset power state
plan/dry-run infrastructure power operations
submit authorized asset-addressed operations
poll PDU jobs
link PDU audit/correlation to ACMS Work/A2A operations
```

ACMS must not know PDU SSH credentials or raw PowerAlert details.

### Agent Runtime Manager / Server Manager

Agent Runtime Manager should also integrate directly with PDU Manager for server/runtime lifecycle use cases such as:

```text
ensure physical host powered on
graceful server shutdown/escalation
reboot/recovery
post-power recovery validation
```

It coordinates Proxmox/OS/runtime state around physical power.

### LLM Manager

LLM Manager should normally request infrastructure capacity/recovery through Server Manager/Agent Runtime Manager.

Read-only direct PDU diagnostics are acceptable.

### Independent agents

Agents operating outside ACMS, e.g. through Telegram, may use PDU Manager directly only with their own least-privilege machine identity and approved asset/action scope.

---

## 10C. PDU Manager work to include in the autonomous sprint

Use the existing PDU repository and extend `/api/v1`.

### PDU Phase 0 — make API documentation truthful

Before consumers depend on it:

- correct stale VM154 production references to VM156;
- reconcile generated/current OpenAPI with `app/api_v1.py`;
- update architecture/current-state docs;
- preserve VM154 as fallback, not normal API endpoint.

No hardware actuation.

### PDU Phase 1 — regression safety net

Add/expand mock-backend tests for:

```text
health
authentication
authorization
PDU/outlet inventory
protected policy
idempotency
jobs
batch validation
rate limiting
audit
backend failure mapping
```

CI must never actuate hardware.

### PDU Phase 2 — asset-addressed API

Implement:

```text
GET  /api/v1/assets
GET  /api/v1/assets/{asset_id}
POST /api/v1/assets/{asset_id}/actions
```

Autonomous systems should request:

```text
MIAM-00119 reboot
```

not:

```text
PDU 151 outlet 9 reboot
```

The PDU Manager resolves the authoritative Git-managed mapping internally.

### PDU Phase 3 — raw-route identity guard

Legacy raw outlet actions should support/require for machine callers:

```json
{
  "expected_asset_id": "MIAM-00119"
}
```

Mismatch must fail before driver dispatch:

```text
409 TARGET_IDENTITY_MISMATCH
```

### PDU Phase 4 — dry-run/action planning

Implement a non-actuating plan endpoint such as:

```text
POST /api/v1/action-plans
```

It should return:

```text
intended asset
resolved PDU/outlet
current state
protected state
caller authorization
required acknowledgements
mapping revision
actuated = false
```

### PDU Phase 5 — capability/version/mapping identity

Expose:

```text
GET /api/v1/capabilities
GET /api/v1/version
```

or equivalent safe health metadata.

Include:

```text
API version
Git/release SHA
backend mode
mapping revision
supported actions
caller permissions
rate limits
```

The \"don't do the work yourself\" rule and the basic lifecycle are auto-injected into every kanban worker's system prompt; this skill is the deeper playbook when you're specifically playing the orchestrator role.", "tags": ["kanban", "multi-agent", "orchestration", "routing"], "related_skills": ["kanban-worker"], "content": "---\nname: kanban-orchestrator\ndescription: Decomposition playbook + anti-temptation rules for an orchestrator profile routing work through Kanban. The \"don't do the work yourself\" rule and the basic lifecycle are auto-injected into every kanban worker's system prompt; this skill is the deeper playbook when you're specifically playing the orchestrator role.\nversion: 3.0.0\nplatforms: [linux, macos, windows]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [kanban, multi-agent, orchestration, routing]\n    related_skills: [kanban-worker]\n---\n\n# Kanban Orchestrator — Decomposition Playbook\n\n> The **core worker lifecycle** (including the `kanban_create` fan-out pattern and the \"decompose, don't execute\" rule) is auto-injected into every kanban process via the `KANBAN_GUIDANCE` system-prompt block. This skill is the deeper playbook when you're an orchestrator profile whose whole job is routing.\n\n## Profiles are user-configured — not a fixed roster\n\nHermes setups vary widely. Some users run a single profile that does everything; some run a small fleet (`docker-worker`, `cron-worker`); some run a curated specialist team they've named themselves. There is **no default specialist roster** — the orchestrator skill does not know what profiles exist on this machine.\n\nBefore fanning out, you must ground the decomposition in the profiles that actually exist. The dispatcher silently fails to spawn unknown assignee names — it doesn't autocorrect, doesn't suggest, doesn't fall back. So a card assigned to `researcher` on a setup that only has `docker-worker` just sits in `ready` forever.\n\n**Step 0: discover available profiles before planning.**\n\nUse one of these:\n\n- `hermes profile list` — prints the table of profiles configured on this machine. Run it through your terminal tool if you have one; otherwise ask the user.\n- `kanban_list(assignee=\"<some-name>\")` — sanity-check a single name. Returns an empty list (rather than an error) for an unknown assignee, so this only confirms a name you're already considering.\n- **Just ask the user.** \"What profiles do you have set up?\" is a fine first turn when the goal needs more than one specialist.\n\nCache the result in your working memory for the rest of the conversation. Re-asking every turn wastes a tool call.\n\n## When to use the board (vs. just doing the work)\n\nCreate Kanban tasks when any of these are true:\n\n1. **Multiple specialists are needed.** Research + analysis + writing is three profiles.\n2. **The work should survive a crash or restart.** Long-running, recurring, or important.\n3. **The user might want to interject.** Human-in-the-loop at any step.\n4. **Multiple subtasks can run in parallel.** Fan-out for speed.\n5. **Review / iteration is expected.** A reviewer profile loops on drafter output.\n6. **The audit trail matters.** Board rows persist in SQLite forever.\n\nIf *none* of those apply — it's a small one-shot reasoning task — use `delegate_task` instead or answer the user directly.\n\n## The anti-temptation rules\n\nYour job description says \"route, don't execute.\" The rules that enforce that:\n\n- **Do not execute the work yourself.** Your restricted toolset usually doesn't even include terminal/file/code/web for implementation. If you find yourself \"just fixing this quickly\" — stop and create a task for the right specialist.\n- **For any concrete task, create a Kanban task and assign it.** Every single time.\n- **Split multi-lane requests before creating cards.** A user prompt can contain several independent workstreams. Extract those lanes first, then create one card per lane instead of bundling unrelated work into a single implementer card.\n- **Run independent lanes in parallel.** If two cards do not need each other's output, leave them unlinked so the dispatcher can fan them out. Link only true data dependencies.\n- **Never create dependent work as independent ready cards.** If a card must wait for another card, pass `parents=[...]` in the original `kanban_create` call. Do not create it first and link it later, and do not rely on prose like \"wait for T1\" inside the body.\n- **If no specialist fits the available profiles, ask the user which profile to create or which existing profile to use.** Do not invent profile names; the dispatcher will silently drop unknown assignees.\n- **Decompose, route, and summarize — that's the whole job.**\n\n## Decomposition playbook\n\n### Step 1 — Understand the goal\n\nAsk clarifying questions if the goal is ambiguous. Cheap to ask; expensive to spawn the wrong fleet.\n\n### Step 2 — Sketch the task graph\n\nBefore creating anything, draft the graph out loud (in your response to the user). Treat every concrete workstream as a candidate card:\n\n1. Extract the lanes from the request.\n2. Map each lane to one of the profiles you discovered in Step 0. If a lane doesn't fit any existing profile, ask the user which to use or create.\n3. Decide whether each lane is independent or gated by another lane.\n4. Create independent lanes as parallel cards with no parent links.\n5. Create synthesis/review/integration cards with parent links to the lanes they depend on. A child created with unfinished parents starts in `todo`; the dispatcher promotes it to `ready` only after every parent is done.\n\nExamples of prompts that should fan out (using placeholder profile names — substitute whatever exists on the user's setup):\n\n- \"Build an app\" → one card to a design-oriented profile for product/UI direction, one or two cards to engineering profiles for implementation, plus a later integration/review card if the user has a reviewer profile.\n- \"Fix blockers and check model variants\" → one implementation card for the blocker fixes plus one discovery/research card for config/source verification. A final reviewer card can depend on both.\n- \"Research docs and implement\" → a docs-research card can run in parallel with a codebase-discovery card; implementation waits only if it truly needs those findings.\n- \"Analyze this screenshot and find the related code\" → one card to a vision-capable profile for the visual analysis while another searches the codebase.\n\nWords like \"also,\" \"finally,\" or \"and\" do not automatically imply a dependency. They often mean \"make sure this is covered before reporting back.\" Only link tasks when one card cannot start until another card's output exists.\n\nShow the graph to the user before creating cards. Let them correct it — including which actual profile name should own each lane.\n\n### Step 3 — Create tasks and link\n\nUse the profile names from Step 0. The example below uses placeholders `<profile-A>`, `<profile-B>`, `<profile-C>` — replace them with what the user actually has.\n\n```python\nt1 = kanban_create(\n    title=\"research: Postgres cost vs current\",\n    assignee=\"<profile-A>\",  # whichever profile handles research on this setup\n    body=\"Compare estimated infrastructure costs, migration costs, and ongoing ops costs over a 3-year window. Sources: AWS/GCP pricing, team time estimates, current Postgres bills from peers.\",\n    tenant=os.environ.get(\"HERMES_TENANT\"),\n)[\"task_id\"]\n\nt2 = kanban_create(\n    title=\"research: Postgres performance vs current\",\n    assignee=\"<profile-A>\",  # same profile, run in parallel\n    body=\"Compare query latency, throughput, and scaling characteristics at our expected data volume (~500GB, 10k QPS peak). Sources: benchmark papers, public case studies, pgbench results if easy.\",\n)[\"task_id\"]\n\nt3 = kanban_create(\n    title=\"synthesize migration recommendation\",\n    assignee=\"<profile-B>\",  # whichever profile does synthesis/analysis\n    body=\"Read the findings from T1 (cost) and T2 (performance). Produce a 1-page recommendation with explicit trade-offs and a go/no-go call.\",\n    parents=[t1, t2],\n)[\"task_id\"]\n\nt4 = kanban_create(\n    title=\"draft decision memo\",\n    assignee=\"<profile-C>\",  # whichever profile drafts user-facing prose\n    body=\"Turn the analyst's recommendation into a 2-page memo for the CTO. Match the tone of previous decision memos in the team's knowledge base.\",\n    parents=[t3],\n)[\"task_id\"]\n```\n\n`parents=[...]` gates promotion — children stay in `todo` until every parent reaches `done`, then auto-promote to `ready`. No manual coordination needed; the dispatcher and dependency engine handle it.\n\nIf the task graph has dependencies, create the parent cards first, capture their returned ids, and include those ids in the child card's `parents` list during the child `kanban_create` call. Avoid creating all cards in parallel and linking them afterward; that creates a window where the dispatcher can claim a child before its inputs exist.\n\n### Step 4 — Complete your own task\n\nIf you were spawned as a task yourself (e.g. a planner profile was assigned `T0: \"investigate Postgres migration\"`), mark it done with a summary of what you created:\n\n```python\nkanban_complete(\n    summary=\"decomposed into T1-T4: 2 research lanes in parallel, 1 synthesis on their outputs, 1 prose draft on the recommendation\",\n    metadata={\n        \"task_graph\": {\n            \"T1\": {\"assignee\": \"<profile-A>\", \"parents\": []},\n            \"T2\": {\"assignee\": \"<profile-A>\", \"parents\": []},\n            \"T3\": {\"assignee\": \"<profile-B>\", \"parents\": [\"T1\", \"T2\"]},\n            \"T4\": {\"assignee\": \"<profile-C>\", \"parents\": [\"T3\"]},\n        },\n    },\n)\n```\n\n### Step 5 — Report back to the user\n\nTell them what you created in plain prose, naming the actual profiles you used:\n\n> I've queued 4 tasks:\n> - **T1** (`<profile-A>`): cost comparison\n> - **T2** (`<profile-A>`): performance comparison, in parallel with T1\n> - **T3** (`<profile-B>`): synthesizes T1 + T2 into a recommendation\n> - **T4** (`<profile-C>`): turns T3 into a CTO memo\n>\n> The dispatcher will pick up T1 and T2 now. T3 starts when both finish. You'll get a gateway ping when T4 completes. Use the dashboard or `hermes kanban tail <id>` to follow along.\n\n## Common patterns\n\n**Fan-out + fan-in (research → synthesize):** N research-style cards with no parents, one synthesis card with all of them as parents.\n\n**Parallel implementation + validation:** one implementer card makes the change while one explorer/researcher card verifies config, docs, or source mapping. A reviewer card can depend on both. Do not make the implementer own unrelated verification just because the user mentioned both in one sentence.\n\n**Pipeline with gates:** `planner → implementer → reviewer`. Each stage's `parents=[previous_task]`. Reviewer blocks or completes; if reviewer blocks, the operator unblocks with feedback and respawns.\n\n**Same-profile queue:** N tasks, all assigned to the same profile, no dependencies between them. Dispatcher serializes — that profile processes them in priority order, accumulating experience in its own memory.\n\n**Human-in-the-loop:** Any task can `kanban_block()` to wait for input. Dispatcher respawns after `/unblock`. The comment thread carries the full context.\n\n## Pitfalls\n\n**Inventing profile names that don't exist.** The dispatcher silently fails to spawn unknown assignees — the card just sits in `ready` forever. Always assign to a profile from your Step 0 discovery; ask the user if you're unsure.\n\n**Bundling independent lanes into one card.** If the user asks for two independent outcomes, create two cards. Example: \"fix blockers and check model variants\" is not one fixer task; create a fixer/engineer card for the fixes and an explorer/researcher card for the variant check, then optionally gate review on both.\n\n**Over-linking because of wording.** \"Finally check X\" may still be parallel with implementation if X is static config, docs, or source discovery. Link it after implementation only when the check depends on the implementation result.\n\n**Forgetting dependency links.** If the task graph says `research -> implement -> review`, do not create all tasks as independent ready cards. Use parent links so implement/review cannot run before their inputs exist.\n\n**Reassignment vs. new task.** If a reviewer blocks with \"needs changes,\" create a NEW task linked from the reviewer's task — don't re-run the same task with a stern look. The new task is assigned to the original implementer profile.\n\n**Argument order for links.** `kanban_link(parent_id=..., child_id=...)` — parent first. Mixing them up demotes the wrong task to `todo`.\n\n**Don't pre-create the whole graph if the shape depends on intermediate findings.** If T3's structure depends on what T1 and T2 find, let T3 exist as a \"synthesize findings\" task whose own first step is to read parent handoffs and plan the rest. Orchestrators can spawn orchestrators.\n\n**Tenant inheritance.** If `HERMES_TENANT` is set in your env, pass `tenant=os.environ.get(\"HERMES_TENANT\")` on every `kanban_create` call so child tasks stay in the same namespace.\n\n## Recovering stuck workers\n\nWhen a worker profile keeps crashing, hallucinating, or getting blocked by its own mistakes (usually: wrong model, missing skill, broken credential), the kanban dashboard flags the task with a ⚠ badge and opens a **Recovery** section in the drawer. Three primary actions:\n\n1. **Reclaim** (or `hermes kanban reclaim <task_id>`) — abort the running worker immediately and reset the task to `ready`. The existing claim TTL is ~15 min; this is the fast path out.\n2. **Reassign** (or `hermes kanban reassign <task_id> <new-profile> --reclaim`) — switch the task to a different profile (one that exists on this setup) and let the dispatcher pick it up with a fresh worker.\n3. **Change profile model** — the dashboard prints a copy-paste hint for `hermes -p <profile> model` since profile config lives on disk; edit it in a terminal, then Reclaim to retry with the new model.\n\nHallucination warnings appear on tasks where a worker's `kanban_complete(created_cards=[...])` claim included card ids that don't exist or weren't created by the worker's profile (the gate blocks the completion), or where the free-form summary references `t_<hex>` ids that don't resolve (advisory prose scan, non-blocking). Both produce audit events that persist even after recovery actions — the trail stays for debugging.\n", "path": "devops/kanban-orchestrator/SKILL.md", "skill_dir": "/home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/skills/devops/kanban-orchestrator", "linked_files": null, "usage_hint": null, "required_environment_variables": [], "required_commands": [], "missing_required_environment_variables": [], "missing_credential_files": [], "missing_required_commands": [], "setup_needed": false, "setup_skipped": false, "readiness_status": "available", "metadata": {"hermes": {"tags": ["kanban", "multi-agent", "orchestration", "routing"], "related_skills": ["kanban-worker"]}}}

{"success": true, "name": "immutable-release-cicd-pipeline", "description": "Build the Git→CI→artifact→staging→production pipeline for an already-running service: releases/ + current-symlink layout, SHA-identified immutable artifacts, protected production environment, automatic application rollback, backend-mode promotion, self-hosted deployment runner. Reference implementations: llm-manager-project-framework (PRs #6–#22) and pdu-marion-ia-usa-project-framework (PRs #7–#19) — the PDU repo's pipeline is PROVEN END-TO-END through production cutover (2026-09-27: VM156 promoted, authorized dispatch → approval → runner → deploy → release ACCEPTED).", "tags": [], "related_skills": [], "content": "---\nname: immutable-release-cicd-pipeline\ndescription: \"Build the Git→CI→artifact→staging→production pipeline for an already-running service: releases/ + current-symlink layout, SHA-identified immutable artifacts, protected production environment, automatic application rollback, backend-mode promotion, self-hosted deployment runner. Reference implementations: llm-manager-project-framework (PRs #6–#22) and pdu-marion-ia-usa-project-framework (PRs #7–#19) — the PDU repo's pipeline is PROVEN END-TO-END through production cutover (2026-09-27: VM156 promoted, authorized dispatch → approval → runner → deploy → release ACCEPTED).\"\n---\n\n# Immutable-Release CI/CD Pipeline Construction\n\n**Trigger this skill when:** asked to make a running service reproducible/deployable from Git, add CI/CD to a capture-style repo, build a staging environment, or implement \"branch → CI → merge → artifact → staging → approval → prod\" for VM-hosted services. NOT for container-native apps (use standard image pipelines) — this is for systemd/nginx/venv services deployed onto VMs.\n\nReference implementations (both `startupteams/*`, 2026-09-27):\n1. `llm-manager-project-framework` PRs #6–#22 — the canonical battle-tested script set (migrations ledger, clean-room bootstrap). **Proven legs:** staging + rollback. Unproven: production promotion (legacy flat layout still in prod).\n2. `pdu-marion-ia-usa-project-framework` PRs #7–#19 — simpler Flask/venv service; **proven END-TO-END through production cutover** (2026-09-27): protected `production` environment live, releases/current layout on the promotion VM (VM156) with the REAL backend, FW-018 config-schema gate inside the transaction, LXC 130 self-hosted runner, and a REAL production deploy (workflow run 36341622843: dispatch → env approval → runner → checksum → transaction → health 5/5 → release ACCEPTED). PDU repo deploy/ scripts (`set-backend-mode.sh`, `validate-read-only.sh`, `validate-config.py`, `logrotate/`, `production-deploy.yml`) are additional templates.\n\nThe deploy/ directory in either repo is canonical — copy and adapt, don't rewrite.\n\n## Production-promotion pattern (pdu repo, proven prep)\n\nWhen promoting a staging VM to Git-managed production of an ALREADY-live service (both prod instances run, old one untouched):\n\n1. Verify gates on the promotion VM BEFORE any cutover (release current, backend mode, read-only state reads vs the old prod host, auth paths, monitor already polling, logrotate). Gate matrix lives in the repo runbook (`docs/CUTOVER_RUNBOOK_*.md`).\n2. Backend/mode switch = first-class guarded mechanism, not a manual edit: systemd drop-in is authoritative + machine-readable state file + fail-safe refusal (e.g. refuses `real` while staging throwaway secrets persist) + removes COMPETING drop-ins that set the same env var.\n3. **systemd drop-in ordering trap:** drop-ins apply in filename order and the LAST one silently wins. A legacy `mock-backend.conf` from install.sh shadowed the new `backend-mode.conf` — service stayed mock while the tool reported \"real\". Fix: current_mode() must scan ALL drop-ins; the switch must neutralize others setting the same var.\n4. Machine-readable mode MUST be observable: add `<mode>` field to the health endpoint (`/health` → `backend_mode`) so monitoring/validation can assert which backend is live. Verification ritual: check `/health` EVERY session before write-path tests (a tool's \"already in mode X\" message is not evidence — the health endpoint is).\n5. Cutover sequence (human-gated): promotion VM onboot=1 FIRST → controlled reboot verification → old prod onboot=0 → old prod ACPI shutdown (never stop-forcibly, never delete) → records. Old host untouched during prep = rollback is `qm start <old>`. Backups: take a one-time PBS snapshot of the promotion VM before cutover (`POST /nodes/<n>/vzdump` with `storage: pbs-marion, mode: snapshot`; poll the UPID task to OK) and verify the old host's newest backup age.\n\n### Production-deploy workflow pattern (proven E2E, pdu repo `production-deploy.yml`)\n\n```yaml\njobs:\n  verify-artifact:      # ubuntu-latest: resolve SHA (see pitfall), build artifact, upload\n  deploy:\n    needs: verify-artifact\n    runs-on: ${{ vars.PDU_DEPLOY_RUNNER_LABEL || 'ubuntu-latest' }}   # self-hosted runner label via repo VARIABLE\n    environment: production                                            # job-level = the approval gate\n    concurrency: { group: production-deploy, cancel-in-progress: false }\n```\n\n- Runner-side mechanics: repo secret `PDU_DEPLOY_SSH_KEY` (dedicated ed25519 keypair) + repo variables `PDU_DEPLOY_RUNNER_LABEL`, `PDU_DEPLOY_HOST`. The job writes the key to a 600 file, scp's the artifact, and ssh-executes the on-box transaction script. **If the runner label var is unset, the job lands on ubuntu-latest which CANNOT reach the private network — make that path print the authorized agent-deploy command and exit 3 rather than faking success.**\n- **Self-hosted runner provisioning (LXC recipe, proven):** Debian 12 CT (template may need `download-url` to the node's local storage first — debian-12-standard_12.12-1_amd64.tar.zst; older version URLs 404 with exit 8), 1C/1G/8G, unprivileged, `nesting=1`, `onboot=1`, static IP. Install runner binaries under a DEDICATED non-root user (`config.sh` refuses sudo; `svc.sh install <user>` as root). Registration token via `POST /repos/<o>/<r>/actions/runners/registration-token` (repo-scoped, ~1h validity). Labels like `<svc>-deploy,<vm>-deploy` drive `runs-on` selection.\n- **Approving your own dispatch is possible via API:** `POST /repos/<o>/<r>/actions/runs/<run_id>/pending_deployments` with `{\"environment_ids\": [<env-id>], \"state\": \"approved\", \"comment\": \"...\"}` — works when the token's user is the configured reviewer (`current_user_can_approve: true` in the GET of the same path). The deployments/statuses endpoint is NOT the approval mechanism (422 on `state: approved`).\n- **Run \"waiting\" ≠ broken:** `gh run list` shows `status: waiting` while the environment approval is pending; `verify-artifact` succeeds while `deploy` waits. Approve via the API above, then the deploy job runs on the labeled runner.\n\n## The architecture (plan §2 of the reference plan)\n\n```\n/opt/<svc>/\n├── releases/<git-sha>/        # immutable trees (app, ops, db, config) — never edited in place\n├── current -> releases/<sha>  # activation = symlink swap (mv -T of current.new)\n└── shared/{logs,state}        # survives releases; release dirs symlink into it\n/etc/<svc>/                    # mutable: secrets/, tls/, generated configs — NEVER inside a release\n```\n\nSystemd units point at `/opt/<svc>/current/...` so activation is a symlink swap and rollback is a symlink swap back. `git pull` into production is banned.\n\n## Pipeline stages\n\n1. **PR → CI on GitHub-hosted runners** (never run arbitrary PR code on self-hosted deployment runners).\n2. **Immutable artifact on main**: `git archive` of the reviewed SHA (untracked junk can't leak in) + `release_manifest.json` (git_sha, build_time, per-file sha256) + `.sha256` sidecar. Secret-scan gate INSIDE the build (sk- patterns, DSNs-with-password, private keys) — fail the build, not just CI.\n3. **Staging CD auto after merge**: self-hosted runner downloads the EXACT CI artifact (workflow_run trigger, checksum-verified, never rebuilt), transfers, runs the release transaction.\n4. **Production via workflow_dispatch + protected environment** (required human reviewer). Must deploy the same artifact SHA that passed staging.\n5. **Release transaction** (deploy-release.sh): preflight → snapshot → stop interference FIRST (the recovery/supervisor service) → install candidate + venv from pinned freeze → candidate validation (compile/tests BEFORE activation) → ledger migrations → activate (symlink swap) → restart services (recovery-class service LAST) → smoke → **auto-rollback on smoke failure**.\n\n## Active placement as a DB source of truth (§21.7 pattern)\n\nWhen a service hard-codes \"which guest is the active deployment per physical host\" as Python constants (VLLM_HOSTS, NODE_MAP), every placement change is a source edit + deploy. Replace the AUTHORITY with a DB table (`active_hosts (physical_host, active_host_ip, updated_at, updated_by)`) + a small module:\n\n- Cached reads (30 s TTL) with **legacy fallback to the constants when the DB is unavailable** — degrade to old behavior, never break (document as TDR).\n- One admin endpoint (`POST /api/hosts/active`, Admin-role gated) promotes a guest via DB update only; run the registry/routing reconcile immediately after so `backend_url`s follow.\n- Keep the constants as the *inventory* + fallback; consumers sort/label by DB-active. `/api/status` reports `active_source: db|legacy` so the mode is observable.\n- Regression tests simulate active = A → B → A purely via DB and assert historical tables (deployment_revisions, ring inventories) stay untouched — switching must never rewrite history.\n- Prove it live on staging: promote → read-back → promote-back with zero source edits.\n\n## Migration discipline\n\n- Ledger table `schema_migrations (version, checksum, applied_at, git_sha)`; forward-only runner (`migrate.py apply/status/verify`).\n- Files named `NNN_destructive_*.sql` are refused by ordinary runs — require maintenance plan + explicit flag + operator backup.\n- Application rollback is only safe across backward-compatible releases; the reference session proved rollback to a release predating an env-var fix fails its own healthcheck — that is correct behavior, document it, don't \"fix\" it.\n- Pre-migration operator backup stays on the host (root-owned 0600); never upload DB dumps to GitHub.\n- Baseline migration for a live system is intentionally EMPTY of DDL — it marks the existing schema as 001 so ordering works for both fresh-install and existing paths.\n\n## Clean-room staging gate (the reproducibility proof)\n\nProvision staging ONLY from: GitHub artifact + documented external secrets + documented external endpoints. Procedure:\n\n1. Clone a prod VM for OS parity, then **wipe everything service-specific**: app trees, units, nginx sites, secret VALUES, TLS, generated configs, logs. A clone that still carries prod secrets or configs is not a clean room.\n2. Renumber identity BEFORE anything else: netplan IP, hostname, machine-id (`systemd-machine-id-setup`). A cloned VM answering on the prod IP is a live incident.\n3. Run `bootstrap-vm.sh <artifact> --staging` (idempotent: apt, Python pin check, venv from freeze, units, nginx + self-signed staging TLS, compat symlinks).\n4. Provision staging-local secrets (fresh values, NOT copies of prod) + isolated DB (local PG on the staging VM is cleaner than schema separation).\n5. Seed safety: mark hosts/targets UNMANAGED/mock so staging recovery can never act on real infrastructure.\n6. Gate: services active, healthz, gated APIs respond (auth gates count as alive), nginx TLS, no restart storm, release manifest observable. Then REBOOT and repeat — persistence counts.\n7. Prove switching/rollback scenarios live (symlink swap back/forward, DB-driven promotion).\n\n## Pitfalls (all found live, 2026-09-27)\n\n- **Compat symlinks**: artifact nests source under `service/` and builds `.venv`, but units/code reference flat `app/`, `venv/`. Bootstrap must create `app -> service/app`, `venv -> .venv` etc. per release. Symptom: units exit 203/EXEC while files obviously exist.\n- **Env-var overrides for hard-coded constants**: every `PG_HOST`-class constant in app code needs an env override (e.g. `LLM_MANAGER_PG_HOST`) with prod default unchanged, or staging can't point at its own DB. Ship the override via systemd drop-in `Environment=`.\n- **Healthcheck timing**: deploy restarts units then healthchecks immediately — a unit in restart backoff (RestartSec=10) reads \"not active\". Healthcheck needs a bounded wait loop (e.g. 10 × 3s) per unit.\n- **Healthcheck route inventory**: a 404 on an optional API route is a release-inventory difference, not a deploy failure — tolerate it. A gated endpoint answering its auth error IS route-alive (the gate working is a PASS).\n- **Healthcheck reads current/ after rollback** — a failing candidate's smoke runs the rollback, and the post-rollback healthcheck correctly reports the OLD release manifest. Don't misread that as a bug.\n- **CI first-run failures to expect**: starlette SessionMiddleware needs `itsdangerous`, FastAPI forms need `python-multipart`, DB-backed tests need `pgserver` (no-Docker PG); `gitleaks-action@v2` requires an org license — use a pinned gitleaks BINARY instead (`detect --source . --no-git`); runner needs `sudo mkdir` for /etc paths; nginx site goes to sites-available + symlink.\n- **QGA file transfer wedges**: large/long guest-exec payloads (multi-KB heredocs, 130KB chunk pushes) wedge the qemu-guest-agent channel — recovery is `qm reset` (services survive; wait for agent ping before next exec). Fallback transfer path: `python3 -m http.server` on the workstation + `curl` from the VM (two-way; remember to kill the server after).\n- **Branch protection + self-merge**: `enforce_admins=false` keeps admin merges possible while CI stabilizes; set `required_approving_review_count` per Jordan's instruction at the time (0 during autonomous sprints, tighten later).\n- **Protected environment via API** (not in repo settings UI workflow): `PUT /repos/{owner}/{repo}/environments/production` with `{\"reviewers\":[{\"type\":\"User\",\"id\":<user_id>}],\"deployment_branch_policy\":{\"protected_branches\":true}}` — reviewer id from `gh api users/<login> --jq .id`. Job-level `environment: production` is what enforces the reviewer gate.\n- **workflow_run-triggered CD**: `deploy-staging.yml` on `workflow_run` of the CI workflow (`conclusion == 'success' && head_branch == 'main'`), runner labels `[self-hosted, marion, <svc>-deploy]`; artifact located via `listWorkflowRunArtifacts` on `run.id`.\n- **Migration runner must fail loudly when it finds nothing**: a runner computing its input dir as `dirname(__file__) + \"/migrations\"` while the script itself lives INSIDE that dir double-nests the path (`migrations/migrations`), finds zero files, and `apply`/`verify` exit 0 with \"verified 0 applied migrations\" — silent success that surfaces later as a confusing downstream deploy failure. Self-referencing dir = `dirname(abspath(__file__))` itself; add a guard: zero input files found ⇒ hard fail (see ops-scripting-pitfalls §9).\n- **Cloned-VM netplan MAC-match trap**: the source VM's netplan pins `match: macaddress: <source-MAC>`; the clone has a NEW MAC so netplan errors `Cannot find unique matching interface for eth0` and the NIC stays DOWN — looks like \"networking broke\" right when you're renumbering. Drop the match block entirely (or substitute the clone's actual MAC from `ip link`) as part of identity renumbering.\n- **Post-handoff async triage**: background subagent results land AFTER the handoff message. Budget one triage pass — cross-check subagent findings against what shipped, fix real gaps (the gpu_power_collector missing-active-host bug was found this way → follow-up PR), and APPEND an addendum section to the handoff rather than rewriting it. Also: a fanned-out subagent can complete with status=failed and NO summary — verify its assigned scope yourself before trusting coverage.\n\n- **CD tooling must never default to prod endpoints** (found live 2026-09-27, PR #20): the transaction and preflight scripts shipped `LLM_MANAGER_PG_HOST=\"${VAR:-<prod-DB-IP>}\"` — a staging migration step then pointed at PROD, stopped only by a missing password. Resolution order for any environment target in tooling: explicit env var → the app unit's systemd `Environment=` (`systemctl show <svc> -p Environment --value`) → local/neutral default (`127.0.0.1`). Prod IPs belong in the documented manifest only (informational), never as code defaults. One missing password is all that separates staging from prod otherwise.\n- **Mirror the app's credential contract in tooling** (PR #21/#22): the app read its DB password from a secrets file (`PG_PW=` line in `/etc/llm-manager/secrets/pg_app_creds`) while the migration runner only knew env vars → `fe_sendauth: no password supplied` on every plain transaction. Tooling must read the SAME source (explicit env override still wins). Python nuance: `os.environ.get(VAR)` returns `''` (not None) for set-but-empty — the transaction script exports an empty password when the unit env lacks it — so `if pw is None` never falls through to the file; use `pw = os.environ.get(VAR) or None`.\n- **Secrets provisioning to a promotion VM, out-of-Git (pdu pattern):** copy the production secrets file as base64-of-FILE from old prod → workstation (in-memory only, never decode/display) → SFTP to target `/tmp` (0600) → on-box `base64 -d | tee` with `chown root:<svcuser> && chmod 640` → shred temp. NEVER via Git, never echo values. Verify on-box with keys-only (`cut -d= -f1`).\n- **PVE qga base64-output mangling:** guest-exec output that looks like base64 gets auto-decoded by the exec wrapper and corrupts binary/b64 payloads (garbage bytes). Workaround: wrap the fetch in marker lines (`echo PDU_MARKER; <cmd>; echo PDU_END`) so the decoder bails, then slice between markers. Also: qga may be inactive on cloud-init VMs — SSH-as-user + passwordless sudo is the more reliable channel; `sudo -S` password piping gets tool-guard-blocked, so provision passwordless sudo or key auth.\n- **Deploy the artifact's own tooling, always:** the installed deploy-release.sh on the target may predate your fixes (staging VM had the old `sha256sum -c` bug; the same script on main was fixed). Unpack the NEW artifact to a scratch dir and run ITS transaction script against its own tarball — then land the tooling fix in Git too (a later release self-heals).\n- **Auth-split validation reality:** API endpoints may deliberately reject emergency/service credentials (V3 §21: `/api/v1` = LLDAP Basic ONLY, emergency web creds → 401 BY DESIGN). A validation script that \"fails\" on 401 may be witnessing correct security behavior — assert the gate, don't fight it. Deep state-read proof may need an in-process probe with the app's own read-only functions instead of HTTP.\n- **Config-schema validation inside the transaction (FW-018 pattern):** run a standalone validator (JSON parse + IPv4 + outlet ranges + duplicate ip/asset/key detection + protected-outlet-must-have-label) at the config-validate stage, wired to the SAME rollback path — a malformed config must abort BEFORE the service restarts. Also runs in CI. `.example.json` files are the CI fixture: label/protection edits flow through tests automatically.\n- **Read-only validator self-check:** a \"never actuates\" script should structurally assert its own innocence (grep itself for forbidden endpoint strings/POSTs; tests assert those strings stay absent) — this catches later edits that sneak a write path in. Careful: comments MENTIONING forbidden endpoints will trip the self-check — phrase comments without literal endpoint paths.\n- **Minimal cloud images lack logrotate** (`logrotate: command not found`) — install it before deploying the rotation config; `logrotate -d` dry-run to verify.\n- *

{"success": true, "name": "self-hosted-llm-gateway", "description": "Deploy and operate self-hosted LLM serving stacks — vLLM fleets behind a LiteLLM gateway with PostgreSQL-backed virtual API keys, per-agent key issuance, model-deployment control, and live dashboards on Proxmox infrastructure.", "tags": [], "related_skills": [], "content": "---\nname: self-hosted-llm-gateway\ndescription: Deploy and operate self-hosted LLM serving stacks — vLLM fleets behind a LiteLLM gateway with PostgreSQL-backed virtual API keys, per-agent key issuance, model-deployment control, and live dashboards on Proxmox infrastructure.\n---\n\n# Self-Hosted LLM Gateway (vLLM + LiteLLM + PostgreSQL)\n\nUse when building or operating an internal LLM control plane: vLLM inference VMs, an OpenAI-compatible gateway with virtual per-agent API keys and budgets, deployment/model management, and telemetry. Pairs with `proxmox-cluster-infrastructure` for the node/guest layer.\n\n## Golden rule: deliver the write-functional core FIRST\n\nThe user was explicitly disappointed by a read-only monitoring shell: *\"I expected a full interface where I can actually change the models hosted by vLLM on these addresses and additionally be able to get API keys to use as backend endpoints.\"* A dashboard without writes is a failed deliverable. Build order:\n\n1. **Gateway serving real completions behind per-agent virtual keys** — issue key → run completion through it → revoke/budget-cap proven. This is the product.\n2. **Model/deployment management** — change model repo / context length / flags per endpoint; drain → apply → restart → re-verify.\n3. **Live dashboard** — probe real endpoints (vLLM `/v1/models`, DB roundtrip); never display fake or cached metrics.\n4. The user corrected a near-miss: MIAM-00110 was OFFLINE so .110 pinged dead but belongs to the node (user: \"10.0.20.110 is definitely used as a static IP for MIAM-00110\"). DHCP pool .190-.250 is off-limits for statics; pick the lowest free gap (e.g. .187), set it via container netplan, then add a Kea reservation (`/api/kea/dhcpv4/add_reservation/` with `{reservation: {subnet: <uuid>, ip_address, hw_address, hostname, description}}` — subnet uuid from `search_reservation/` rows) so the pool can't lease it later.\n- **OPNsense scripted access (no SSH, no API keys):** web-session login works — POST /index.php with ALL hidden CSRF inputs + `usernamefld`/`passwordfld` + `login=1`, then `X-CSRFToken` header (regex from any /ui page JS: `setRequestHeader\\(\"X-CSRFToken\",\\s*\"([^\"]+)\"`) for `/api/...` MVC calls. Kea lease search: `/api/kea/leases4/search/`; reservations: `/api/kea/dhcpv4/search_reservation/` / `add_reservation/`. Spend analytics (v0.6.0), RDMA ring config option (v0.7.0), and facility rows (v0.7.1) all landed this way — prefer wiring new capabilities into the web UI with supporting endpoints, not CLI-only paths. He personally reviews dashboards and reports what he sees, so keep displayed caveats accurate.\n- **Power telemetry sources (Jordan rule, 2026-09-10):** GPU NVML = the primary dynamic signal; Emporia = independent facility/whole-circuit view; **PDUs are reserved for control — never poll PDU network endpoints for power telemetry** (\"we do not want to block the PDUs with network traffic\"; at most a once-daily spot read). Local-model $/token accounting: GPU watts × effective seasonal electricity rate (componentized records), rolling 7/30-day windows, reported in both GPU-attributed (primary basis) and server-total (high-end margin) variants.\n- **\"Continue until finished; if you hit a roadblock, work on other related tasks\"** — when the user authorizes full-plan execution, keep every phase on the todo list, dispatch independent workstreams in parallel (delegate_task), and never end a turn with only a plan or a partial report. Deferred ≠ stopped. Parallel background builders write progress-note files (e.g. `<topic>-progress.md`) in the session work dir — long remote builds (model downloads, cmake) must run under nohup on the remote machine, and the orchestrator integrates results when builders finish.\n- **Narrate elimination progress in long autonomous debugging chains (Jordan, 2026-09-12):** a multi-attempt RDMA/vLLM debugging loop with visible tool calls but no intermediate commentary prompted a mid-chain \"are you stuck in a loop? Do you need some help?\" — Jordan reads silence as stuck. When an authorized autonomous run enters its 3rd+ fix attempt, each final assistant message should carry a one-line \"attempt N failed on X, eliminated, moving to Y\" progress ledger (not just tool-call logs), so he can tell a productive elimination chain from a loop at a glance.\n- **Verify \"missing access\" claims with verbose tracing before reporting a roadblock** — an empty-output SSH run was once misread as \"no node access\" when the root password sat in `~/.miam_root_pass` all along; the fix was a better runner, not an escalation to the user (see pitfalls).\n- **Hashing ≠ encryption — say so when the user sees crypto tooling.** The user asked \"we don't want to encrypt any of this storage stuff... can you explain what you're doing?\" when bcrypt/argon2 appeared mid-build. Rule: when a password hash, TLS cert, or sealed-secret step shows up in output, add one plain-language line (\"this is a login check, not storage encryption; nothing on disk is encrypted; no performance impact\"). Never let crypto vocabulary pass unexplained — the user reads it as hidden storage-encryption scope creep.\n\n## Web control plane: auth + role mapping (built 2026-09-09)\n\n- **Login session flow**: FastAPI `SessionMiddleware` (key in a 600 secret file) + LDAP\n  bind-login via the service bind account, then verify the user by binding AS the user DN.\n  Role = strongest `llm-manager-vm114-*` group. Write APIs gated on role sets\n  (Operator→restarts; Admin/Security-Admin→key issuance + unit edits).\n- **Granting new users manager roles (LLDAP):** LLDAP silently REJECTS LDAP-protocol\n  group-member writes (bind succeeds, then `session terminated by server`; no change\n  lands) — group membership must go through the LLDAP GraphQL API on the LLDAP host\n  (:17170): `POST /auth/simple/login {username,password}` → bearer token →\n  `POST /api/graphql {\"query\":\"mutation { addUserToGroup(userId: \\\"<uid>\\\" groupId: <int>) { ok } }\"}`.\n  Group IDs are ints (lookup with `{ groups { id displayName } }`); the manager's\n  admin group is `llm-manager-vm114-Admin` (id 8 on this cluster). Verify the role\n  landed by re-running `ldap_login()` from the app venv — the group search result is\n  the proof, not the mutation response.\n- **nginx route table must enumerate EVERY route the app serves** — `/login`, `/logout`,\n  `/admin`, `/api/`, `/healthz` — anything unlisted falls into `location / → 302 /admin` and\n  produces an infinite redirect loop (browser: ERR_TOO_MANY_REDIRECTS; tests: 302 chains).\n  After adding app routes, `nginx -t && systemctl reload nginx`.\n- **LiteLLM admin API verbs**: `/key/generate`, `/key/block`, `/key/delete`, `/key/update`\n  are POST, but **`/key/list` AND `/key/info` are GET** (POST returns 405 Method Not Allowed).\n  - `GET /key/list` returns `{\"keys\": [<raw token STRINGS>], \"total_count\": …}` — NOT\n    objects. Iterating with `k.get(...)` throws `'str' object has no attribute 'get'`.\n    Fetch per-key metadata with `GET /key/info?key=<token>` (query param, not JSON body);\n    the useful fields live under `info` (`key_alias`, `spend`, `max_budget`, `blocked`,\n    `expires`, `models`).\n  - Truncated 502 responses from `/key/generate` usually mean LiteLLM can't reach its Postgres — check\n  the litellm journal for `httpx.ConnectError` before touching app code. Then diagnose the\n  unreachable-DB in order: (1) port REFUSED while ICMP/SSH work + service verified\n  listening on the DB CT = **IP collision with a physical host** (compare `ip neigh <ip>`\n  MAC vs the CT's real NIC MAC — this happened: a DB CT sat on a cluster node's corosync\n  address; see proxmox skill's network audit reference), (2) pg_hba/grants only after\n  connectivity is proven.\n- **DB relocation runbook** (moved CT across IPs): grep every consumer for the old IP\n  (`grep -rn \"10.0.20.<old>\" /opt/<app>/ /etc/<app>/`), sed-repoint app `PG_HOST` and\n  gateway `database_url` in the same pass, `systemctl restart` BOTH services, then prove\n  with the service ports (app login page + gateway `/v1/models` 401-with-bad-key = healthy\n  upstream), not just `systemctl is-active`. An unrepointed stale reference survived one\n  move and was only caught during a later network audit.\n- **Audit everything to a JSONL file** (`/var/log/llm-manager/audit.log`): login ok/denied +\n  reason, key issue/block/revoke, unit edits, restarts. The audit log is what makes\n  debugging auth failures possible (\"unknown user\" vs \"invalid credentials\" vs \"no role\"\n  each pinpoint a different broken layer).\n- **Guest control agent pattern** (no arbitrary shell): fixed-command script\n  `/opt/llm-control/llm-control.sh {status|get-unit|set-unit|restart|logs|nvidia}` per vLLM\n  guest; gateway host holds a dedicated SSH keypair (`/etc/llm-manager/secrets/keys/`),\n  pub key appended to each guest's `authorized_keys` via the owning node + base64\n  `qm guest exec` (stdin is NOT forwarded — see proxmox skill). Unit edits: validate\n  `[Service]` + `ExecStart=` present, keep `.bak.<ts>`, `daemon-reload` WITHOUT restart —\n  the UI instructs the operator to restart separately (save ≠ apply).\n- **Keep the relay path documented for remote administration.** ssh_run.py (root+pexpect)\n  reaches Proxmox NODES only; the gateway VM takes a different path: land script on\n  owning node → `scp ubuntu@<vm-ip>` → `ssh … \"sudo bash /tmp/x.sh\"`. A session burned a\n  round-trip trying to ssh_run.py straight into the VM (`Permission denied (publickey)`).\n  Before driving a guest, check which path applies (node = root/password, VM =\n  node-relay with the baked-in key).\n\n## Deployment recipes\n\n- **Never invent vLLM flags.** Recover the battle-tested serve command from the target guest's root `~/.bash_history` (last working variant) and encode it in a systemd unit. Environment: `HF_HOME`, `VLLM_USE_FLASHINFER_SAMPLER=0`, `VLLM_USE_DEEP_GEMM=0` are typical for this fleet.\n- **Per-deployment profiles.** The same logical model name can expose different `max_model_len` (observed 262144 vs 32768 on identical model IDs). Store concurrency/context envelopes per deployment; never assume homogeneity.\n- **Routable requires proof:** `/v1/models` returns the expected model AND a real chat completion succeeds — health endpoint alone is insufficient.\n- Qwen thinking models may emit reasoning inline unless `--reasoning-parser qwen3` is set — check parser flags when output contains \"Thinking Process...\". Thinking traces consume `max_tokens`: a \"max_tokens: 40\" test request can be fully consumed by the reasoning preamble before any answer text appears.\n- **Reasoning-parser fleet rollout pattern (verified 2026-09-10):** canary on ONE host first — add `--reasoning-parser qwen3` next to `--tool-call-parser qwen3_xml` in the unit, restart, verify `/v1/models` after the ~3-4 min reload, THEN confirm (a) `reasoning` field is separated and `content` is clean, (b) tool calling still works (parser combo must return proper `tool_calls`, not raw XML). Only then roll the rest, **staggered with per-host health gates** and automatic rollback if a host fails to come up in ~5 min. Unit NAMES differ per host (`vllm.service` vs `vllm-qwen.service`) — detect the RUNNING unit (`systemctl list-units --state=running | grep vllm`) instead of assuming; grep matching \"already set\" against the wrong unit file gives false positives. Clients get clean content via `\"chat_template_kwargs\": {\"enable_thinking\": false}` (e.g. `content: '27'` end-to-end through the gateway); with thinking ON, max_tokens is consumed by the reasoning preamble first.\n- **Hybrid-Mamba models (Qwen3.x family): `--max-num-seqs` must fit the Mamba cache.** Every concurrent sequence holds one Mamba cache block; 16 GB cards at `--gpu-memory-utilization 0.92` fit only ~92 blocks, so vLLM's default 256 aborts during CUDA-graph capture with `RuntimeError: max_num_seqs (256) exceeds available Mamba cache blocks (92)` and the unit crash-loops. Fix: `--max-num-seqs 64` (comfortable headroom, still generous for Hermes load). Block count scales with free VRAM per replica: dense Qwen3.8-27B at TP2/DP3 on 20 GB cards at 0.90 util had 163 blocks → `--max-num-seqs 128` succeeded after the default 256 aborted with the identical error. Never copy the 3080 hosts' turboquant KV/attention flags onto smaller-VRAM GPUs without qualification.\n- **llama.cpp as a serving engine (VM149, proven 2026-09-11):** the fleet is no longer\n  vLLM-only. llama.cpp `llama-server` (OpenAI-compatible :8000, `--alias <logical-name>`\n  sets the served model name) registers in LiteLLM the same way: `openai/<alias>` +\n  `api_base: http://<ip>:8000/v1` + dummy api_key. **Flag trap:** vLLM-style flags do\n  NOT exist in llama.cpp — `--mlock off` is invalid (`--mlock`/`--no-mlock` are the\n  llama.cpp forms, and no-mlock is already the default; one vLLM-habit flag caused a\n  9,740-restart crash-loop). Crash-loop from an invalid flag shows\n  `error: invalid argument: --<flag>` in journalctl within 1 s of each start — check\n  the unit's ExecStart against the ENGINE's flag set, not a generic one. Control agent\n  on such a host must be retargeted to the new unit name AND support full `set-unit`\n  (validate `[Service]`+`ExecStart=`, `.bak` backup, write, daemon-reload, NO\n  auto-restart — save ≠ apply), plus start/stop/version/models. Verify the agent's\n  get-unit/set-unit/restart roundtrip from VM114 before telling the user the UI works.\n- **Crash-loop signature:** systemd reports `active` while `NRestarts` climbs, worker PIDs change between checks, and nothing listens on :8000. Don't keep polling the port — pull `journalctl -u vllm --no-pager | grep -iE \"error|RuntimeError\"` f

Formal retention period still Jordan's call (v2 §17.1).\n- **SNMP_* cleaned from Git surfaces (FW-016):** example + test fixtures dropped the vars; the REAL\n  values remain in production `secrets.env` files (removal there = maintenance-window item; never\n  in Git either way).\n- **Legacy code removed (FW-017):** `app/legacy_app_direct.py` deleted (PR #15); pre-V3 bootstrap\n  script `deploy/scripts/pdu-control-bootstrap.sh` retained but header-marked HISTORICAL (embedded\n  config has wrong protection set [3,4,5,6,12] + SNMP app — never run it).\n- **Flask session signing key derives from the emergency creds** (`pdu-control-v3:{WEB_USER}:{WEB_PASS}`\n  SHA-256) — rotating WEB_PASS rotates the session signing key; existing sessions invalidate.\n- **GitHub state (2026-09-27):** protected `production` environment exists (required reviewer:\n  jordatech, protected-branch policy) + `production-deploy.yml` + ADR-0005 (runner LXC 130,\n  PROPOSED awaiting Jordan); no branch-protection rule on `main` itself yet; CI green.\n  Auto-rollback PROVEN (real drill: ROLLBACK_OK with config restore; found+fixed the\n  symlink-only-restore defect).\n\n## Cold-cycle recipe (node + enclosure qualification)\n\n1. Cleanly shut the node down first (PVE API `POST /nodes/<node>/status {\"command\":\"shutdown\"}`;\n   the agent terminal hardline-blocks shell `shutdown` — see proxmox skill). Wait for down.\n2. OFF enclosure outlet first, then host outlet (each its own request_id).\n3. Poll `/api/status` cached_states until both OFF and `busy:false` (PDU SSH driver is slow;\n   state may lag ~60s).\n4. Soak ≥60s (90s used), then ON enclosure first, ON host second.\n5. Host typically back on the PVE API ~2 min after ON; poll `GET /nodes/<node>/status` until\n   200 with `uptime` > 30s, then give systemd ~60-75s before pct/zpool checks.\n\n## Access paths (post-cutover)\n- Production web UI: `https://10.0.20.156/` (VM156; TLS via nginx; :80 redirects). Login = LLDAP session + emergency root form (`mode=emergency`, `e_user`, `e_pass`). Creds staged in VM154-provisioned secrets.\n- Old prod UI (fallback only): `https://10.0.20.154/` — VM154 is powered off; do not expect it to answer.\n- AI/API: `https://10.0.20.156/api/v1` — HTTP Basic with LLDAP creds ONLY (emergency creds rejected by design, V3 §21). Test agent `miam_0154_pdu_agent` (pdu-ai-agent + pdu-operator; NO override). Creds `~/.pdu_lldap_service_creds` (0600).\n- In-VM shell: SSH as `jordatech` (10.0.20.156) + passwordless sudo — the reliable path (qga is inactive on VM156; the PVE-API guest-exec path works for VM154 when it is booted).\n- TLS cert: self-signed, DER sha256\n  `137308d592260184fd75b7a555e27f61332a8c7ffe5feb1f58d1c5553b2221a9`.\n\n## Architecture (what runs where)\n- systemd `pdu-control`: gunicorn 1 worker × 4 threads, **127.0.0.1:5000**\n  ONLY. nginx terminates TLS :443 (config\n  /etc/nginx/sites-available/pdu-control). nftables `inet pdu_filter` allows\n  80/443/22 from 10.0.20.0/24 + 10.0.10.0/24, drops 80/443 otherwise.\n- Modules: `app.py` (UI + legacy /api routes) → `action_service.py` (single\n  action path + invariants + idempotency) → `app_runtime.py` (locks, audit,\n  worker subprocess via `pdu_worker.py` → `pdu_ssh_direct.py` pexpect\n  PowerAlert SSH driver — DO NOT touch the driver).\n- `auth_lldap.py`: bind-check + `(member=<userDN>)` group search via service\n  bind `svc-pdu-manager-vm154`; 60s group cache (revocation lands ≤60s).\n\n## LLDAP facts (10.0.20.101)\n- LDAP protocol port **3890** (NOT 17170 — that's web/GraphQL UI).\n- Base DN `dc=example,dc=com`; user DN `uid=<user>,ou=people,...`.\n- NO memberOf: search `ou=groups,dc=example,dc=com` w/ `(member=<userDN>)`.\n- No GraphQL password mutation: use ldap3 ModifyPassword extop bound as admin.\n- Groups: pdu-viewer(11) pdu-operator(12) pdu-admin(13) pdu-ai-agent(14)\n  pdu-ai-admin-override(15).\n\n## Hard invariants (never weaken)\n- Protected outlets (153: [3,4,5,6,9]): OFF forbidden for EVERYONE\n  (PROTECTED_OFF_FORBIDDEN), even admin_override/root. Override = REBOOT only,\n  requiring admin_override + reason + acknowledge_protected_device (+ for\n  153/9: acknowledge_controller_may_go_offline). Expected final state ON.\n- Protected reboot = native PDU `4- Cycle Load` (atomic, PDU-committed). Never\n  implement OFF-then-ON in the controller.\n- 153/9 reboot: writes `/var/lib/pdu-control/pending_reboot.json`, commits the\n  cycle, VM dies mid-verify, boot reconciler verifies/recovers ON + audits.\n\n## API usage pattern (agents)\nGET /me → GET outlet → one UUID Idempotency-Key per intended action → POST\naction → poll GET /jobs/{id} → verify state. Never retry with a NEW request id\non timeout (idempotency replays the original job; new id = new power event).\nRate limits: 120 GET/min, 20 POST/min per identity (429 + Retry-After).\nIdempotency persists across restarts (/var/lib/pdu-control/idempotency.json).\n\n## Pitfalls learned\n- secrets.env must stay 640 root:pducontrol (chmod 600 breaks the service).\n- gunicorn must stay --workers 1 (runtime structures are per-process).\n- Dashboard cold-load does 3 serial PDU SSH state reads (~30-60s) — normal.\n- Config JSON at /etc/pdu-control/config.json is the protection source of\n  truth; backup dir /root/pdu-control-backups/20260910-222303/.\n- Rollback: `qm rollback 154 pre_lldap_api_upgrade_20260910` on miam-00133.\n- Deploy via guest-exec needs ≤12KB base64 chunks per write (596 broken pipe\n  on big single posts).\n- LLDAP GraphQL: `user(userId:)` not `user(id:)`; introspect mutations.\n\n## Live power-cycle test protocol (learned 2026-09-27, authorized 00119 test)\n\nThe authorized MIAM-00119 test validated the full actuation path through VM156 and produced durable wiring knowledge:\n\n- **Verified mapping:** `151:9 = MIAM-00119` (PVE node, Dell 7010). OFF dropped the node (~10s), ON restored it, PDU REBOOT (native Cycle Load) cleanly rebooted it. All three legs audit-logged.\n- **⚠️ Power-cut latency is NOT instant:** an outlet can read OFF at the PDU relay for ~25s+ before the attached node actually loses power (capacitors/PSU holdup). A fast ping loop showing \"still up\" right after OFF does NOT prove a wiring mismatch — wait 30s+ minimum before concluding anything.\n- **⚠️ 152:3 = miam-00135 = a LIVE Proxmox node** (hosts VM114 LLM-Manager, VM120, CT122 ACMS). Never dispatch \"wiring probes\" to 152:3. Recovery verified 2026-09-27 (TDR-0002).\n- **Pre-dispatch discipline:** quote the exact `ip + outlet + label` triple from `config/examples/config.example.json` in the dispatch reason; verify target identity against the authoritative config BEFORE the 202, and prefer the least-consequential asset for any probe.\n- Driving actuation via the emergency session: curl with cookie jar (`-c/-b`), `POST /api/action` JSON, then verify via in-process `read_state` (the app's venv python) — job polling from an emergency session 401s on `/api/v1`.\n", "path": "devops/pdu-manager-vm154/SKILL.md", "skill_dir": "/home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/skills/devops/pdu-manager-vm154", "linked_files": null, "usage_hint": null, "required_environment_variables": [], "required_commands": [], "missing_required_environment_variables": [], "missing_credential_files": [], "missing_required_commands": [], "setup_needed": false, "setup_skipped": false, "readiness_status": "available"}

DB restore is REFUSED outside an open\n  transaction.\n- `deploy/validate-release.sh --stage app|full --strict-build\n  --expected-rev <rev> [--feature-smoke <cmd>]`.\n- `deploy/smoke-test.sh [base-url]` — standalone operator smoke (not a\n  release gate): backend probes run IN-CONTAINER via `compose exec -T acms-app\n  python` + urllib with exact status asserts (health 200, version 200, unauth\n  agents 401, Work UI 303 via proxy) and exits 1 on any failure. Do NOT\n  reintroduce host-side `127.0.0.1:8000` probes — the app publishes no host\n  ports (nginx is the sole ingress by design). Reuse\n  `_base_context` + shared constants in new UI routes (a locally re-declared\n  delivery string diverged from the Work UI wording and a version-empty footer\n  appeared; single source of truth fixes both). Home \"not yet implemented\"\n  list must track what the backend actually maintains.\n- **Tooling-self-replacement caveat:** when a target tip CHANGES\n  `deploy/release.sh` (or rollback/validate scripts), the running transaction\n  checks out the new tree mid-flight while bash still reads the old script\n  from disk — unproven territory. Never put secrets in release metadata.\n- Feature-specific smoke hooks via `ACMS_FEATURE_SMOKE` env (plan §11).\n\n## Live CT122 quirks (validated 2026-09-26)\n\n- Git checkout is at `/opt/acms/repo` (NOT `/opt/acms`); `.env` at\n  `/opt/acms/.env` (0600, key names only — never echo values).\n- **Compose v2 container names are `acms-<service>-1`** — bare\n  `docker exec acms-app` FAILS (\"No such container\"). Always\n  `docker compose -f deploy/compose.yaml --env-file /opt/acms/.env exec -T\n  <service>` (service-name agnostic). If a manual hop stage dies on a bare\n  name AFTER copying the maintenance conf, the host\n  `deploy/reverse-proxy/nginx.conf` is already switched —\n  `git checkout -- deploy/reverse-proxy/nginx.conf` BEFORE retrying, or the\n  retry double-applies maintenance content.\n- No `pg_dump`/`psql` on the CT host — run inside the postgres container:\n  `docker compose -f deploy/compose.yaml --env-file /opt/acms/.env exec -T\n  postgres pg_dump -U acms -Fc acms`.\n- No `curl` in the app image — probe with `docker exec acms-acms-app-1\n  python -c \"import urllib.request; ...\"` on 127.0.0.1:8000.\n- nginx allowlists 10.0.10.0/24 + 10.0.20.0/24 — validate via the VM's own IP\n  `https://10.0.20.122` with `curl -k` (self-signed; MARION has NO home.arpa\n  DNS — raw IPs everywhere).\n- `/version` returns `{version, git_sha, git_sha_short, build_time}` — the\n  git_sha identity check catches wrong-image states instantly. Run it after\n  ANY container recreation.\n- LLDAP 10.0.20.101:3890, base dc=example,dc=com; groups acms-admin/16\n  (jordatech), acms-workers/17, acms-observers/18.\n\n## Docker/Compose pitfalls (from the live drill — see references file)\n\n- **Single-file bind mounts pin the inode**: swapping the file on disk is\n  invisible to the container; HUP reload then serves stale config forever.\n  nginx now uses a directory mount (`./reverse-proxy:/etc/nginx/conf.d`) and\n  the maintenance template is named `*.conf.template` so the include glob\n  never loads it. Never re-introduce a single-file conf mount.\n- **`docker compose up -d <svc>` recreates depends_on services too** and can\n  bring the app back with the default image (`acms-app:local`). Pin\n  `ACMS_APP_IMAGE_TAG=<tag>` on EVERY compose up in tooling.\n- **Never command-substitute `compose build`** — its stdout is the build log\n  and pollutes captured variables (`invalid tag`). Send build output to\n  `>&2`; compute the tag, never capture it.\n- **pipefail + `grep -c` false positive**: `grep -c` exits 1 when the count\n  is 0; capture the count into a variable with `|| true`, compare numerically.\n- Recovery from stuck maintenance mode: `up -d --force-recreate\n  reverse-proxy`, then re-pin the app image, then verify via `/version`.\n\n## Bootstrap hop (chicken-and-egg first deploy)\n\nThe first deployment of the release tooling cannot use the tooling.
